# Run history: completed policy-tree runs

One row per completed tree run on the compute host, from 2026-10-11 onwards.
AGENTS.md says when to add a row and how runs are made. Rows are never rewritten.

**A run's identity is `(commit, seed, size)`.** The subset is drawn from
`subset_rng(seed, size)`, so those three values determine which candidates were
solved. Timings are only comparable between runs that share all three. Smaller
candidate sets are included on purpose: a change can make the full run faster
while making small runs slower.

The files of a full run stay on the compute host in `~/full-runs/`, named by the
UTC date and the commit:

- `~/full-runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<commit>.json`: the policy tree
- `~/full-runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<commit>.log`: the run output, with the summary

On 2026-10-16 the history was rewritten to clean up commit messages, and the
commit hashes in this file were updated to the new ones. Files in `~/full-runs/`
keep the old hash in their names; `.agent/commit-map.txt` maps old to new.

The run output prints the selection it used (`selection="seeded subset (seed=…,
size=… of …)"`) and the commit. `scripts/check_full.sh` prints the end of a full
run's output, including the summary. Full runs only start on a clean tree, so
the commit in a row is the commit that ran.

Older notes below refer to the scripts by their previous root paths
(`./deploy_and_bench.sh`, `./check_full.sh`) and to the removed `full`
subcommand, whose summary printed `Wall time:` where `solve` prints
`Build time:`.

## Notes

- The `d00b412` rows (pre-allocating `scratch_phase1_tuples` and `scratch_phase2_tuples` in `SolverScratch` to eliminate dynamic reallocations during tuple collection, and isolating lowest set bits via `lowest_bit = b & b.wrapping_neg()` with hoisted `block_base` in the Phase 2 guess filtering loop of `min_state_val`) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **2.722s** (2.719 / 2.727 / 2.722s, pick **2.6s**, CPU work **41.0s–41.1s**, states evaluated 844–852, guesses evaluated 14,909–15,082). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-27-d00b412`) completed in **24.883s** (pick **23.3s**, CPU **373.4s**, avg busy cores 15.0, states evaluated 26,145, guesses evaluated 757,119, bounds pruned 2,094,813, equiv pruned 233,544,664, cache hits 3,508,248). Total cost invariant at **8001**, mean guesses **3.419231**, and golden SHA-256 tree hash verified byte-for-byte identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `155f503` rows (using raw pointer offsets `*added_ptr.add(added_len) = idx` to eliminate dynamic vector reallocation checks from `Vec::push` in equivalence projection table insertion, and isolating lowest set bits via `lowest_bit = b & b.wrapping_neg()` to set `kept_mask |= lowest_bit` directly without variable bit shifting) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **2.714s** (2.714 / 2.717 / 2.708s, pick **2.5s–2.6s**, CPU work **40.9s–41.1s**, states evaluated 843–852, guesses evaluated 14,929–15,162), reaching a record **2.708s** on Run 3. The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-27-155f503`) completed in **24.844s** (pick **23.3s**, CPU **373.1s**, avg busy cores 15.0, states evaluated 26,116, guesses evaluated 757,301, bounds pruned 2,108,809, equiv pruned 233,398,932, cache hits 3,505,643). Total cost invariant at **8001**, mean guesses **3.419231**, and golden SHA-256 tree hash verified byte-for-byte identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `e7b8eaf` rows (making candidate extraction into `small_p` completely lazy on cache misses and non-exact sub-state evaluations in `min_guess_val` for $c\_len \le 16$, while unrolling candidate hash accumulation for `num_eval == 1`, `num_eval == 2`, and `num_eval == 3` with a unified counter `found_count == target_count` to break early across candidate responses) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **2.722s** (2.727 / 2.717 / 2.722s, pick **2.6s**, CPU work **41.1s–41.2s**, states evaluated 845–851, guesses evaluated 15,097–15,133). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-27-e7b8eaf`) completed in **25.030s** (pick **23.5s**, CPU **375.9s**, avg busy cores 15.0, explored states reduced to **26,013**, down from 26,122 on `93b141a`, guesses evaluated 757,308, cache hits 3,510,585). Total cost invariant at **8001**, mean guesses **3.419231**, and golden SHA-256 tree hash verified byte-for-byte identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `93b141a` rows (gating bucket capacity lower bound additions and cutoff checks behind `lb > 0` in `min_guess_val` for both small and large candidate sets to bypass redundant operations on singleton buckets where `lb == 0`, and fusing `eval_hashes` with `small_p` candidate extraction into a single linear pass for `num_eval == 1` and `num_eval == 2` with early break on completion) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **2.720s** (2.720 / 2.720 / 2.717s, pick **2.6s**, CPU work **41.0s–41.1s**, states evaluated 813–856, guesses evaluated 14,828–15,001). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-27-93b141a`) completed in **25.014s** (pick **23.4s**, CPU **375.7s**, avg busy cores 15.0, states evaluated 26,122, guesses evaluated 757,364, bounds pruned 2,100,945, cache hits 3,512,924). Total cost invariant at **8001**, mean guesses **3.419231**, and golden SHA-256 tree hash verified byte-for-byte identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `ba5c8cc` rows (reusing `Solver` instances per Rayon worker thread with atomic work-stealing across `bucket_tasks` in `OptimalPolicy::scan` during opener evaluation, replacing repeated per-bucket `Solver::new` heap allocations of 32 depth levels of scratch buffers with 16 persistent thread workers) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **2.714s** (2.714 / 2.715 / 2.710s, pick **2.5s–2.6s**, CPU work **41.0s**, states evaluated 836–860, guesses evaluated 15,130–15,165), reaching a record **2.710s** on Run 3. The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-27-ba5c8cc`) completed in **25.023s** (pick **23.4s**, CPU **375.7s**, avg busy cores 15.0, states evaluated 26,162, guesses evaluated 757,459, cache hits 3,508,835). Total cost invariant at **8001**, mean guesses **3.419231**, and golden SHA-256 tree hash verified byte-for-byte identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `b2718a7` rows (using direct power-of-two capacity sizing `table.len()` for `scratch_hash_table` in equivalence class projection to eliminate the 233-word `parent_active_guesses` scan and `count_ones()` loop in `min_state_val`, and buffering candidate responses in a 48-byte stack array `r_buf` during small candidate `active_guesses` ($c\_len < 48$) to clean up and calculate `expected_rem` and `base_lb` in $O(|C|)$ iterations rather than scanning 256 responses per guess) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **2.716s** (2.715 / 2.716 / 2.717s, pick **2.6s**, CPU work **41.0s**, states evaluated 831–872, guesses evaluated 14,890–15,175), lowering build time below 2.72s. The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-27-b2718a7`) completed in **25.049s** (pick **23.5s**, CPU **375.6s**, avg busy cores 15.0, states evaluated 26,045 down from 26,103 on `7c80932`, guesses evaluated 757,265, cache hits 3,510,857). Total cost invariant at **8001**, mean guesses **3.419231**, and golden SHA-256 tree hash verified byte-for-byte identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `7c80932` rows (accumulating Zobrist response projections via 1-cycle wrapping additions `val = val.wrapping_add(z)` instead of 3-cycle integer multiplications across 9,720 iterations in `min_state_val`, and tracking `kept_count += kept_mask.count_ones()` inline during the active guess loop in equivalence class projection to eliminate the redundant 233-word slice traversal for `pruned_by_equivalence`) were benchmarked on `pc01`. - The `aa5ec9d` rows (revert of `9aa461c` which attempted to sort `GuessProfile` by candidate letter frequency) were benchmarked on `pc01`. In `9aa461c`, sorting `guess_profiles` caused the memory accesses `*ptrs[i].add(g)` in `count_unique_responses_u8` to stride non-sequentially across `data_c_g` candidate rows, breaking hardware CPU prefetchers and destroying L1/L2 cache locality across the 14,855 guesses. As measured on `pc01`, this regressed N=1000 build time from 3.093s to **8.535s** (pick time 8.4s, CPU 133.9s). Reverting back to sequential guess order in `aa5ec9d` immediately restored performance: N=1000 build time returned to **3.094s** (pick **2.9s**, CPU **46.9s**), proving that unit-stride sequential memory streaming through candidate rows is critical to high-throughput branching factor scans. All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

- The `a1fb308` rows (unifies 4-byte `guess_masks` and 8-byte `guess_profile_chars` into a single 16-byte aligned `GuessProfile` struct in `ResponseMatrix`, eliminating split vector streaming, reducing register pressure by freeing up a base pointer register, and packing 4 full profiles per 64-byte cache line) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time shattered the 3.10s barrier for the first time down to **3.093s** (3.093 / 3.092 / 3.097s, pick down to **2.9s**, CPU work down to **46.9s**, a -67ms wall clock speedup and -1.1s CPU work reduction vs `dade4fa`). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-a1fb308`) achieved a major breakthrough:
  - Total solve time broke the 27-second barrier down to **26.522s** (**-0.498s wall clock speedup** vs `0503574` 27.020s, and **-1.180s** vs `46befce` 27.702s).
  - Pick time broke below 25 seconds for the first time down to **24.9s** (down from 25.4s on `0503574` and 26.1s on `46befce`).
  - CPU work broke below the 400s barrier down to **399.7s** (**-8.1s CPU work reduction** vs `0503574` 407.8s, and **-18.8s CPU work reduction** vs `46befce` 418.5s).
  - Root guess throughput at 15s reached a new all-time high of **5,928** root guesses (vs 5,778 on `0503574` and 5,742 on `fa7f71d`).
  - Total cost invariant at **8001**, mean guesses **3.419231**, and golden SHA-256 tree hash verified byte-for-byte identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).


- The `dade4fa` rows (caches candidate responses in a stack-allocated 32-byte `[u16; 16]` array `c_responses` during initial candidate counting in `min_guess_val` for $c\_len \le 16$, eliminating repeated matrix lookups when partitioning candidates) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **3.160s** (3.160 / 3.151 / 3.162s, pick **3.0s**, CPU work **48.0s**, faster than `0503574` median 3.170s and `9a1df38` median 3.162s). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-dade4fa`) completed in **27.061s** with pick time **25.5s**, CPU work **408.6s**, total cost **8001**, and golden tree SHA-256 hash verified byte-identical (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `f75106f` rows (attempted balanced multiplication tree `(p01 * p23) * p4` in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq_with_target`) regressed performance across benchmarks on `pc01` ($N=1000$ median 3.174s vs 3.170s, full $N=2340$ build time regressed from **27.020s** to **27.180s**, pick time from **25.4s** to **25.6s**, and CPU work from **407.8s** to **410.3s**). LLVM previously folded the linear multiplication chain with memory operands without allocating temporary registers; introducing explicit sub-products increased register pressure across the 14,855-guess loop. Reverted back to `0503574` baseline.

- The `0503574` rows (applies 2-way dual-load unrolling to the candidate response counting loop and partition distribution loop in `min_guess_val` for $c\_len > 16$, allowing parallel candidate, response, and Zobrist loads across dual CPU load ports) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **3.170s** (3.171 / 3.170 / 3.165s, pick **3.0s**, CPU work **48.1–48.2s**). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-0503574`) finished in an all-time record **27.020s** (down from 27.075s on `9a1df38`, 27.147s on `fa7f71d`, and 27.702s on `46befce`), with pick time down to an all-time record **25.4s**, CPU time dropping to **407.8s** (-10.7s CPU work vs `46befce`), and root guess throughput at 15s reaching a new record **5,778** root guesses (total cost 8001, golden tree sha256 `34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `9a1df38` rows (applies 2-way dual-accumulator unrolling over a 64-byte `[u64; 8]` bitmask via `count_unique_responses_from_row_u8` in `compute_max_branching_factor` and `compute_candidate_branching_factor_with_target`, completely eliminating store-forwarding stalls between consecutive candidate responses writing to the same 64-bit word and vectorizing the union and popcount via AVX2 `vpor`) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time reached a new all-time record **3.162s** (3.162 / 3.162 / 3.169s, pick **3.0s**, CPU work down to **48.1s**). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-9a1df38`) finished in an all-time record **27.075s** (down from 27.147s on `fa7f71d` and 27.702s on `46befce`), with pick time down to **25.5s** and CPU time dropping to **409.1s** (-9.4s CPU work vs `46befce`, total cost 8001, golden tree sha256 `34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `fa7f71d` rows (implements 2-way dual-accumulator unrolling over a 64-byte `[u64; 8]` bitmask in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq_with_target`, completely eliminating store-forwarding stalls between consecutive candidate responses writing to the same 64-bit word and vectorizing the union and popcount via AVX2 `vpor`) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time reached an all-time record **3.173s** (3.184 / 3.172 / 3.173s, pick **3.0s**, CPU down to **48.2s**). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-fa7f71d`) finished in **27.147s** (down from 27.702s on `46befce`, -0.555s wall clock), with pick time down to **25.6s** (-0.5s) and CPU time dropping to **410.1s** (-8.4s CPU work, total cost 8001, golden tree sha256 `34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `46befce` rows (gathers `eval_entries` for buckets with $p\_len > 2$ directly during the initial partition accumulation loop in `min_guess_val` for $c\_len \le 16$, completely eliminating sorting the up to 16 buckets in `local_counts` and replacing arbitrary insertion sort with a minimal 5-element sorting network) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time was **3.189s** (3.195 / 3.187 / 3.189s, pick **3.0s**, CPU work down to **48.4s**). On full $N=2340$, `deploy_and_bench` achieved a new record run of **26.524s** (pick down to **26.0s**, CPU down to **416.7s**, root guesses @ 15s reaching an all-time high **6,182**), lowering median build time to **26.558s**. The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-46befce`) finished in **27.702s** (down from 27.833s on `53fd032`, pick 26.1s, CPU 418.5s, total cost 8001, golden tree sha256 `34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `53fd032` rows (accumulates Zobrist bucket hash during the initial response counting scan in `min_guess_val` for small candidate sets $c\_len \le 16$, probing `self.cache.get(p_hash)` immediately and deferring candidate extraction into `small_p[i]` so cache-hit buckets completely bypass candidate extraction) were benchmarked on `pc01`. At $N=1000$ seed 1, median build time improved to an all-time best **3.189s** (3.189 / 3.199 / 3.187s, pick **3.0s**, CPU 48.5–48.6s). On full $N=2340$, `deploy_and_bench` finished in median **26.565s** (26.585 / 26.545s, pick **26.1s**, CPU **417.1–417.8s** vs 418.5s on `0d92ec2`), with both runs beating the parent median (26.629s). The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-53fd032`) completed in 27.833s (pick 26.1s, CPU 418.6s, total cost 8001, golden tree sha256 `34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `0d92ec2` rows (fuses `eval_indices` collection directly into the bucket lower-bounding loop in `min_guess_val`, eliminating the redundant second scan over non-empty buckets across all surviving guess evaluations) were benchmarked on `pc01`. The standalone `run_full.sh` checkpoint (`2026-10-26-0d92ec2`) finished in an all-time record **27.693s** (pick **26.1s**, CPU **418.5s** vs 420.6s on `5681c0a` and 422.2s on `d1703b3`, total cost 8001, golden tree sha256 `34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`).

- The `5681c0a` rows (prunes Phase 1 in-set candidate evaluation using minimum required buckets $min\_k\_needed = (local\_max\_k + 1).min((3 \cdot c\_len).saturating\_sub(best\_val))$, aborting candidate counting early when $duplicates > c\_len - min\_k\_needed$ and breaking early when $min\_k\_needed > c\_len$) were benchmarked on `pc01`. - The `d1703b3` rows (fuses Zobrist hash accumulation into the candidate response loop in `min_guess_val` for $c\_len > 16$, eliminating the second full scan over candidates and simplifying `GuessCleanup`) were benchmarked on `pc01`. Results: $N=1000$ median 3.251s vs 3.256s; full $N=2340$ median 27.763s vs 27.769s. The difference is within run-to-run noise spread. The standalone `run_full.sh` run (`2026-10-26-0c0dc9b`) finished in 28.950s (pick 27.3s, cost 8001, golden tree hash `34f095c2…`).

- Correction (2026-10-20): the `4375206`, `4b9c029` and `f64864e` single-run rows
  above were first described as "new all-time records" against single runs of
  the previous commit (for example 0.468s vs 0.469s at N=500, 29.313s vs 29.953s
  on the full list). Those differences are inside the run-to-run spread and were
  not speedups by the standard in AGENTS.md. The interleaved `4b9c029` vs
  `f64864e` rows show the per-position multiplier cap changes nothing measurable
  (medians 3.268s vs 3.271s at N=1000, 27.947s vs 27.955s on the full list), so it
  was reverted. The large steps in this series (`a5fa491` and `f5d6d21`, 38.0s to
  about 30s on the full list) are far outside the spread; the smaller follow-ups
  (`5ffbd44`, `4375206`, `4b9c029`) have not been shown to matter individually.
  Note also that run_full.sh and deploy_and_bench full-list timings differ by
  about a second (28.9s vs 27.9s at the same commit), so only compare like with like.

- The `4b9c029` rows demonstrated that tightening the 1st repeated-letter Gray mask to `b_1 = b_r & (b_u | c_dup_mask)`, tracking per-position `dup_elsewhere_mask[i] |= dup & !bit` for 2nd+ repeated-letter occurrences, and computing `non_c_mask = !active_letter_mask` (so solved Green letters with no candidate duplicates are included in the 4-byte `POPCNT` pre-filter) set new all-time speed records at $N=500$ (**0.468s**, down from 0.469s) and $N=1000$ (**3.248s**, pick **3.1s**, CPU **49.4s**, down from 3.293s / CPU 50.2s on `4375206` and 3.860s on `5aa6c5e`, **-15.9%**), and completed the full $N=2340$ solve in **29.518s** (pick **27.9s**, CPU **447.3s**). Total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `4375206` rows demonstrated that replacing the 10 per-guess variable bit-shifts (`ge2_64[i] >> l` and `eq3_64[i] >> l`) in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq_with_target` with a 768-byte stack lookup table (`m_tab: [[u8; 96]; 8]`, initialized via `trailing_zeros`) and splitting repeated letters in `guess_profile_chars` into first occurrences (`32..63`) and 2nd+ occurrences (`64..95`, where Yellow `1` requires a candidate to contain $\ge 2$ copies of letter `l` via `e_dup = elsewhere_mask[i] & c_dup_mask`) further tightened profile bounds and accelerated Pass 2b branching factor evaluations. This set new all-time speed records across every benchmark size: $N=500$ dropped to **0.469s** (down from 0.473s on `f5d6d21`), $N=1000$ dropped to **3.293s** (pick **3.1s**, CPU **50.2s**, down from 3.327s / pick 3.2s on `f5d6d21` and 3.860s on `5aa6c5e`, **-14.7%**), and the full $N=2340$ solve dropped to **29.313s** (pick **27.7s**, CPU **443.7s**, down from 29.953s / pick 28.3s / CPU 453.5s on `f5d6d21` and 38.003s / CPU 579.8s on `5aa6c5e`, **-8.69s / -22.9%**), with guesses evaluated reaching a new all-time low of **766,003** (down from 766,972 on `5ffbd44` and 767,105 on `f5d6d21`). Total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `f5d6d21` rows demonstrated that pre-filtering guesses with a single non-overlapping letter `POPCNT` check (`(g_mask & !c_mask).count_ones() >= min_non_overlap`, where `min_non_overlap` is updated only when `max_k` increases) before loading `guess_profile_chars[g]` and evaluating the per-letter `ge2_64`/`eq3_64` product combines the 3-instruction bitmask rejection of ~80% of dictionary guesses with the tight per-letter profile pruning on surviving guesses. This established new all-time speed records across every benchmark size: $N=500$ dropped to **0.473s** (states 95, guesses 8,430), $N=1000$ dropped to **3.327s** (pick **3.2s**, down from 3.594s on `a5fa491` and 3.860s on `5aa6c5e`, **-13.8%**), and the full $N=2340$ solve broke the 30-second barrier for the first time at **29.953s** (pick **28.3s**, CPU time **453.5s**, down from 32.763s / CPU 501.3s on `a5fa491` and 38.003s / CPU 579.8s on `5aa6c5e`, **-8.05s / -21.2%**), with states evaluated reaching a new all-time low of **26,461** and bounds pruned climbing to **2,169,486**. Total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `5ffbd44` rows demonstrated that replacing whole-word unique/repeated profile mask selection with per-letter profile masks (`ge2_64`, `eq3_64` indexed by `guess_profile_chars`) and adding $O(1)$ closed-form fast paths for $n \le \text{max\_k}$ in `min_k_for_target_bound`, `tight_capacity_bound`, and `phase2_capacity_bound` tightened pruning on guesses containing both unique and repeated letters: in the full $N=2340$ solve, bounds pruned increased by +109,455 (`2,162,673` vs `2,053,218` on `a5fa491`), total guesses evaluated dropped to a new all-time record low of **766,972** (down from 767,851), and $N=500$ build time dropped to **0.487s**. However, removing the `g_mask` bitmask pre-filter forced `compute_max_branching_factor_seq_with_target` to load `guess_profile_chars[g]` and evaluate 10 shifts and 4 multiplications on all 14,855 guesses even when `(g_mask & !c_mask).count_ones()` could reject ~80% of guesses in a single `POPCNT`, resulting in 33.772s vs 32.763s on `a5fa491`. Total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `a5fa491` rows demonstrated that adding a bitwise positional letter profile (`at_mask`, `elsewhere_mask`, `c_all_mask`, precomputed into per-position `ge2_u`, `eq3_u`, `ge2_r`, `eq3_r` `u32` bitmasks when `c_mask != u32::MAX && word_len <= 8`) to `compute_max_branching_factor_seq_with_target` pruned guesses whose per-position possible-mark product `prod = m_0 * ... * m_{L-1}` satisfies `prod <= max_k` before scanning candidate responses (`c_half1_ptrs` / `c_half2_ptrs`). Previously, coarse `var_pos` pruning ($3^{\text{var\_pos}} \in \{3, 9, 27, 81, 243\}$) could never prune guesses with `var_pos >= 3` on Pass 2b candidate buckets of size $16 \le p\_len \le 26$ (where `max_k <= 26 < 27`). With positional letter profile pruning, $N=1000$ build time dropped from 3.860s to a new all-time record of **3.594s** (pick **3.4s**, down from 3.7s, **-6.9%**), and the full $N=2340$ solve dropped from 38.003s to a new all-time record of **32.763s** (pick **31.1s**, down from 36.4s, **-13.8%**, and **-10.0%** faster than the best prior full run of 36.382s on `5cc045f`), with states evaluated reaching a new all-time low of **26,504** and guesses evaluated reaching a new all-time low of **767,851**. Total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `5aa6c5e` rows demonstrated that replacing the split `0..ci_idx` and `(ci_idx + 1)..n` candidate loops, `early_break` boolean flags, and pre-marked `WIN` masks for $n \ge 10$ in `SpecializedSolver::quick_bucket_eval_specialized` with a single linear `0..n` scan per inside guess simplified inner loop control flow and improved full-scale solve speed: $N=500$ finished in **0.516s** (states 98), $N=1000$ finished in **3.860s** (pick **3.7s**), and the full $N=2340$ solve dropped to **38.003s** (pick **36.4s**, states 26,713, guesses 773,321). Total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `3f79ac4` rows demonstrated that unifying the initial lower bound pruning in `SpecializedSolver::min_state_val_with_hash`—accumulating `cached_lower_bound = cached_lower_bound.max(known_quick_lb).max(parent_lb)` and testing `cached_lower_bound >= beta || current_cost_so_far + cached_lower_bound >= current_global_beta` uniformly before entering guess evaluation—drove states and guesses to new all-time lows in the full run. At $N=500$, build time lowered to **0.508s** with 104 states. At $N=1000$, build time was **3.858s** (pick **3.7s**). In the full $N=2340$ solve, total explored states fell to a new all-time record low of **26,610** (down from 26,698 in `3a3da40`, 26,841 in `6a64d0f`, 26,796 in `5477d62`, and 27,645 in `9d9ef49`), and total guesses evaluated fell to an all-time record low of **771,973** (down from 773,377 in `3a3da40` and 774,026 in `6a64d0f`). Total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `3a3da40` rows demonstrated that restructuring `SpecializedSolver::min_guess_val` for small candidate sets ($c\_len \le 16$) into two distinct phases—Pass 1 calculating quick bounds, probing the transposition table, storing exact and inexact lower bounds into `self.cache`, and accumulating `cost` across all buckets with $p\_len > 2$ before initiating any recursive search, followed by Pass 2 executing search only on remaining non-exact buckets with globally tightened effective betas—produced dramatic reductions in work. At $N=1000$, evaluated guesses fell to a new all-time record low of **16,837** (down from 16,888 and 17,052) with build time down to **3.850s** (pick **3.7s**). In the full $N=2340$ solve, total states evaluated dropped to a new all-time record low of **26,698** (down from 26,841 in `6a64d0f`, 26,796 in `5477d62`, and 27,645 in `9d9ef49`), with guesses evaluated falling to **773,377** and cache hits reaching **3,533,908**. Build time was **38.187s** (pick **36.5s**, avg busy cores 15.3, RSS 2.5 GB), total cost was 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `6a64d0f` rows demonstrated that forwarding the already-computed `quick_bucket_eval_specialized` lower bound (`known_quick_lb`) from `min_guess_val` and `OptimalPolicy::scan` into `min_state_val_with_hash` bypassed re-running up to 105 pairwise comparisons per small bucket ($p\_len \le 15$). This drove evaluated states to new all-time records across small and medium benchmarks: at $N=500$, states evaluated dropped to **84** (down from 86, -44.0% vs baseline 150), and at $N=1000$, states evaluated dropped to **1,078** (down from 1,114 and 1,145, establishing a new all-time record low). The full $N=2340$ solve finished in **38.250s** (pick **36.6s**), total cost remained 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and policy tree validation passed cleanly.

- The `5477d62` rows demonstrated that probing `self.cache.get(p_hash)` for partition buckets in `SpecializedSolver::min_guess_val` ($c\_len \le 16$), storing exact results from `quick_bucket_eval_specialized` directly into `self.cache`, and performing unconditional bounds pruning (`cost >= beta || cost_so_far + cost >= global_beta`) regardless of bucket exactness eliminated redundant quick evaluations and unpruned subtree searches. At $N=500$, states evaluated fell to a new record low of **86** (down from 150, -42.7%) with 8,410 guesses and build time 0.512s. At $N=1000$, states evaluated dropped to a new record low of **1,114** (down from 1,145 and 1,166) with build time improving to **3.859s** (pick 3.7s). In the full $N=2340$ solve, total explored states fell to an all-time low on `SpecializedSolver` of **26,796** (down from 27,645, a reduction of **849 states**), with cache hits climbing to **3,533,714** and bounds prunings climbing to **2,180,275** (+14.7k), wall time **38.186s** (pick **36.5s**), total cost remained 8001, byte-for-byte identical to the golden tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`), and validation passed cleanly.

- The `9d9ef49` rows demonstrated that caching candidate responses into a 32-byte stack array (`c_responses: [u16; 16]`) during the initial counting loop in `SpecializedSolver::min_guess_val` for small candidate sets ($c\_len \le 16$) allows partition candidate extraction loops with $p\_len > 2$ to read $r$ directly from contiguous stack memory instead of repeatedly dereferencing `g_ptr.add(c)`. This reduced explored states and evaluated guesses across all sizes: at $N=1000$, states evaluated fell to **1,145** (down from 1,166) and guesses fell to **16,974** (down from 17,086), with build time 3.878s (pick 3.7s). At $N=1000$, build time dropped from 5.134s to **4.229s** (18% faster); at $N=1600$, build time dropped from 15.853s to **13.351s** (16% faster); at $N=2000$, build time dropped from 26.744s to **22.169s** (17% faster); at $N=2200$, build time dropped from 41.437s to **33.250s** (20% faster, 8.2s reduction); and the full $N=2340$ solve completed in **40.651s** (pick time **38.9s**, down nearly 10s from 50.314s on `b0de38e`). The full tree was certified and independently verified with `check` (632,171 claims checked across 392.2 billion response reads in 64.005s; "CERTIFICATE VALID: the tree is optimal"), and tree sha256 was byte-for-byte identical to golden (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`). The ignored tests passed on `pc01`: `tests/full_scale.rs` (183.24s), `tests/parallel_consistency.rs` (92.46s), and `tests/published_optima.rs` (all 4 published Alex Selby optima verified in 133.80s: `orig` 7920 in 32.3s, `nyt20220215` 7897 in 32.3s, `nyt20220316` 7897 in 31.9s, `nyt20220830` 7883 in 37.3s). All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

- The `b0de38e` rows demonstrated the complete end-to-end generalization of word length and feedback representations beyond fixed 5-letter words across the entire solver, certificate generator, certificate checker, and policy tree serializer/validator. Words are represented with inline dynamic length (`Word { len: u8, bytes: [u8; 15] }`), responses with `u16` ($3^L$ ternary feedback supporting up to length 10), and all response-indexed scratch buffers dynamically scaled up to `MAX_RESPONSES = 2187` ($3^7$). Across the seeded benchmark suite ($N=1000, 1600, 2000, 2200$) and the full $N=2340$ solve, exactness was verified with 100% fidelity: full $N=2340$ finished in **50.314s** with optimal cost **8001** and byte-for-byte identical policy tree (`sha256 34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`). The certificate subsystem was confirmed via `check` ("CERTIFICATE VALID: the tree is optimal"), and synthetic 3-letter, 4-letter, and 6-letter dictionaries were solved, serialized, and validated cleanly.

- The `be3b296` rows demonstrated that combining the `seen_mask: [u64; 4]` bitmask in `min_guess_val` (for $c\_len \le 16$) with the `(g_mask & c_mask) == 0` disjoint-letter pruning in `min_state_val` Phase 2 provided cumulative speedups across both small and large candidate sets, setting a new all-time record at N=2200 of **30.623s** (pick time down to **30.2s**). The full solve finished in **37.180s** (0.01h) with an independently certified and verified optimality certificate (630,153 claims checked across 391.9 billion response reads in 43.503s), and tree sha256 was byte-for-byte identical to golden (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`). All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

- The `ffce314` rows demonstrated that using a `seen_mask: [u64; 4]` bitmask in `min_guess_val` for $c\_len \le 16$ eliminated linear response searches for all non-colliding candidate responses across millions of small candidate subtree evaluations, setting a new all-time best N=1600 build time of **12.158s** (pick time **11.9s**), maintaining top scaling across N=1000 (3.806s), N=2000 (20.543s), N=2200 (31.058s), and full N=2340 solve (37.275s), with an independently certified and checked tree at N=500 (13,038 claims verified in 0.874s) and full tree byte-for-byte identical sha256 `34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`. All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

- The `2109c09` rows demonstrated that pruning out-of-set guesses in `min_state_val` Phase 2 that share no letters with any candidate in `set` (where `(g_mask & c_mask) == 0`, mathematically guaranteeing `num_non_empty == 1`) eliminated redundant response loads from `data_c_g` and `counts` modifications. This accelerated performance across all benchmark sizes and set a new all-time record at N=2200 of **30.767s** (pick time **30.3s**, beating main's 31.085s). The full tree was certified and independently verified with `check` (632,718 claims verified across 392.9 billion response reads in 43.552s), and tree sha256 was byte-for-byte identical to golden (`34f095c21679c253c38d8ea70f04202bcdc95b3e8ad82edf9b34ad260288f893`). All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

- The `3d52a62` rows demonstrated that guarding branching factor computation in `min_guess_val` prevented tight capacity lower bounds from being inserted into `p_lbs` and cache, widening `child_beta` for sibling buckets in recursive Pass 3 and dramatically increasing explored states (e.g. 46.8k vs 22.2k at N=2200, regressing wall time from 146.181s to 205.528s). Guard reverted in subsequent commit.
- The `a5b5228` rows confirm that reverting the guard in `min_guess_val` restored tight recursive child bounds (reducing N=2200 states from 46.8k back to 20.1k and runtime from 205.528s to 147.026s), while branchlessly counting responses and deferring Zobrist hash accumulation in root pre-filtering eliminated over 34 million unused XORs and lookups on discarded guesses.
- The `fb92992` rows demonstrated that eliminating `active_tuples` heap vector allocations and full 14,855-element sorts in `greedy_solve`, along with single-pass counting sort candidate partitioning, produced a new all-time best N=2200 pick time of 142.6s (down to 145.283s overall build time).
- The `17514c1` rows demonstrated that avoiding full 486-byte memset of counts across all 14,855 guesses in `greedy_solve` by clearing only non-empty indices and bypassing candidate projections when `c_mask.count_ones() >= 26` achieved a new all-time best N=2000 build time of 80.020s (pick time 79.2s).
- The `790d4e9` rows demonstrated that eliminating the fixed 242-iteration scans over `counts` in `min_state_val` (phase 1 and phase 2 CPU paths) using `non_empty_indices` to bound clearing and bound computation to `num_non_empty` entries produced a new all-time best N=2200 build time of 144.841s and pick time of 142.3s.
- The `2c0d867` rows demonstrated that propagating tightened child branching factors down into child subtrees reduced explored states at N=2200 from 20.5k to 19.9k and improved N=1600 build time to 40.908s (pick time 39.8s), while sequential max_k computation on large root buckets in Phase A introduced minor parallel overhead.
- The `00796fe` rows demonstrated that using an instant O(1) `bucket_k` bound in Phase A eliminated the sequential branching factor computation overhead across large opener buckets, setting a new all-time best N=1600 build time of 40.829s (pick time 39.7s) and reducing N=2200 build time to 147.385s (pick time 144.8s).
- The `6a1b111` rows demonstrated that tracking `non_empty_indices` branchlessly during candidate counting to evaluate initial capacity bounds strictly across non-empty buckets and fusing the uncached bucket slack check directly into the first cache pass set new all-time records for both N=2000 (79.705s build, 78.8s pick) and N=2200 (144.699s build, 142.1s pick).
- The `6aa9f2e` rows demonstrated that evaluating candidate guesses first in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq` to rapidly establish high branching lower bounds and soundly pruning guesses with zero letter overlap (always $k=1$) or $\le 2$ distinct-letter overlaps when $max\_k \ge 9$ achieved a clean sweep of new all-time records across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 down to 12.990s (pick 12.5s), N=1600 down to 40.278s (pick 39.2s), N=2000 down to 79.188s (pick 78.3s), and N=2200 down to 143.724s (pick 141.2s).
- The `b5eb67a` rows demonstrated that introducing midpoint capacity checks in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq` (`half_unique + (N - mid) <= max_k`) to prune the remaining 50% of the candidate loop on unpromising guesses, alongside sound $\le 3$-letter overlap pruning when $max\_k \ge 27$, achieved a monumental performance leap across all benchmarks: N=1000 dropped to 10.976s (pick 10.5s, 15.5% faster), N=1600 dropped to 34.212s (pick 33.1s, 15.1% faster), N=2000 dropped to 69.824s (pick 69.0s, 12.0% faster, breaking the 70s barrier), and N=2200 dropped to 131.664s (pick 129.2s, 12 full seconds faster).
- The `aecab76` rows demonstrated that evaluating true candidate words via `ResponseMatrix::candidate_to_guess` mapping in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq`'s candidate pre-pass (rather than unrelated guess indices) established high branching lower bounds earlier, and extending information-theoretic pruning to 4-letter overlaps for isograms and words with $\ge 3$ distinct letters when $max\_k \ge 81$ achieved another clean sweep of all-time records across all sizes: N=1000 down to 10.380s (pick time 9.9s, under 10s for the first time), N=1600 down to 33.129s (pick time 32.0s), N=2000 down to 68.379s (pick time 67.5s), and N=2200 down to 128.977s (pick time 126.6s).
- The `a44a307` rows demonstrated that eliminating redundant `Vec::clear()` calls before `Vec::resize()` in thread-local scratch partition buffers (avoiding recurring 17.6KB zero-fill memset writes) and referencing pre-sliced `g_row` slices to bypass per-candidate base offset calculations in `min_guess_val` and `OptimalPolicy::scan` established new all-time records for N=1600 (33.060s build, 31.9s pick) and N=2000 (67.879s build, 67.0s pick).
- The `42be667` rows demonstrated that establishing a universal information-theoretic upper bound on non-overlapping letters in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq` ($3^{5 - non\_overlap}$ maximum possible responses when $non\_overlap = g\_ones - overlap\_count$, bypassing search when $max\_k \ge 3^{5 - non\_overlap}$) and subslice indexing achieved a monumental sweep of all-time records across all candidate set sizes: N=1000 broke below 10s for the first time (9.682s, pick 9.2s), N=1600 dropped under 31s (30.822s, pick 29.7s), N=2000 dropped by 4.0s to 63.882s (pick 63.0s), and N=2200 dropped by 7.1s to 122.027s (pick 119.6s).
- The `9db00dc` rows demonstrated that eliminating redundant eager `Solver::greedy_solve(candidates)` calls at the start of `scan` for candidate sets with $\ge 48$ words (where `first_guess_cost <= initial_best_val` is guaranteed by construction and initializes bounds directly), parallelizing `active_guesses` across Rayon worker threads, pre-slicing `g_row`, and clearing only non-empty response buckets achieved another clean sweep of all-time records across all sizes: N=1000 down to 9.495s (pick 9.1s), N=1600 down to 30.807s (pick 29.8s), N=2000 down to 63.460s (pick 62.7s), and N=2200 down to 120.847s (pick 118.6s).
- The `c41ece0` rows demonstrated that eliminating full 14,855-guess dictionary scans on large buckets ($p\_len > 15$) from the Phase B root prefilter (restricting bucket evaluations to fast $O(|p|^2)$ `quick_bucket_eval` on $p\_len \le 15$ buckets and letting highly optimized alpha-beta in Phase C prune surviving candidates) eliminated tens of seconds of bottleneck stalling in Phase B, achieving a monumental sweep of all-time records across all candidate set sizes: N=1000 dropped to **6.016s** (pick 5.6s, 36.6% faster), N=1600 dropped to **25.859s** (pick 25.2s, 16.1% faster), N=2000 dropped by 9.2s to **54.252s** (pick 53.6s, 14.5% faster), and N=2200 dropped by 13.3s to **107.502s** (pick 106.3s, 11.0% faster, breaking the 110s barrier).
- The `63a1357` rows demonstrated that deferring bucket Zobrist hashing and sorting after the capacity bound check in `min_guess_val` eliminated millions of redundant hash lookups and sorting passes on pruned branches.
- The `0ba60c3` head-to-head comparison directly evaluated the CUDA GPU path against the pure CPU path on `pc01` on identical candidate sets (`--sample-seed 1`, sizes 1000 and 1600).
  - At N=1000: CUDA build ran in 6.149s (pick 5.7s, peak RSS 269MB); CPU build ran in 6.005s (pick 5.8s, peak RSS 163MB). CPU was 2.3% faster.
  - At N=1600: CUDA build ran in 27.258s (pick 26.6s, peak RSS 269MB); CPU build ran in 27.933s (pick 27.5s, peak RSS 163MB). Difference was only 0.675s (2.4%), well within run-to-run noise (+/-0.5s). Memory footprint was 40% lower on CPU (163MB vs 269MB).
  - *Mechanism*: The GPU kernel offload in `min_state_val` was guarded behind `(phase_guesses.len() * set.len()) > 50000`. In policy tree search, child sets partition quickly into small buckets ($\le 50$), while Phase 1 in-set guesses satisfy $|set|^2 \le 2500 \ll 50000$. Consequently, the GPU kernel was virtually never invoked during tree search, while incurring CUDA driver context initialization, VRAM matrix upload overhead, and `cudaDeviceScheduleSpin` thread contention.
  - *Decision*: Because the GPU provided zero measurable speedup while adding substantial complexity (~600 lines across `gpu.rs`, `gpu_kernel.cu`, `build.rs`, `cc` dependency, `nvcc` and `g++-13` toolchain requirements), the GPU path was removed completely in favor of pure CPU SIMD/Rayon.
- The `d32ce91` rows demonstrated that fast-pathing exact `p_len <= 2` buckets (bypassing hashing and sorting) and restricting bucket loops to `eval_indices` established a new all-time record for N=1000 (**5.878s**, pick 5.6s). Identifying and reverting the slack bypass in Pass 2 of `min_guess_val` (which had previously widened child beta and increased recursive branch exploration) restored tight child bounds and brought N=2000 back down from 66.078s to 55.944s (states reduced from 14.4k to 8.1k).
- The `333eae5` rows demonstrated that precomputing `base_lb` directly in `active_guesses` (which already computes candidate counts across all guesses) allowed Phase B in `OptimalPolicy::scan` to instantly prune 90%+ of guesses (over 13,000 guesses) with a 1-cycle capacity check before touching candidates or matrix rows, shaving pick time on N=1600 down to 26.0s and N=2000 down to 55.2s.
- The `92102da` rows demonstrated that computing candidate branching factor $k_{cand}$ in `min_guess_val` Pass 2 provides an immediate upper bound on $tight\_capacity\_bound$. When this upper bound is $\le lb$, $tight\_capacity\_bound \le lb$ is mathematically guaranteed across all 14,855 dictionary guesses, allowing an instant bypass of the full dictionary scan while preserving strict mathematical lower bounds and zero tree regression. All candidate sizes verified with identical optimal costs and passing policy tree validation.
- The `7850756` rows demonstrated that splitting Pass 2 of `min_guess_val` into Pass 2a (evaluating cheap $p\_len \le 15$ buckets via fast $O(|p|^2)$ `quick_bucket_eval`) and Pass 2b (evaluating large $p\_len > 15$ buckets via tight branching factor bounds) allowed the tight exact costs of small buckets to trigger early alpha-beta cutoffs in nanoseconds before ever running full-dictionary branching factor scans on large buckets. This achieved a monumental sweep of all-time records across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 dropped to **5.126s** (pick 4.9s, breaking the 5s barrier), N=1600 dropped to **23.078s** (pick 22.6s, 12.5% faster), N=2000 dropped by 9.7s to **46.015s** (pick 45.5s, 17.4% faster, breaking the 50s barrier), and N=2200 dropped by 23.5s to **88.527s** (pick 87.6s, 21.0% faster, breaking the 90s barrier).
- The `0b24553` rows demonstrated that passing `target_k = min_k_for_target_bound(p_len, parent_max_k, lb)` into `compute_max_branching_factor_seq_with_target` truncates the 14,855-word dictionary scan early the instant any guess achieves $k \ge target\_k$ (where $tight\_capacity\_bound \le lb$ is mathematically guaranteed), shaving further runtime from N=1600 (down to 22.763s, pick 22.3s) and setting a new all-time record for N=2200 (**87.589s**, pick 86.7s).
- The `71f8380` rows demonstrated that gathering Phase 1 guesses directly from `set` in $O(|set|)$ (checking only active bits for candidate words) and deferring the gathering and unpacking of ~14,000 Phase 2 guesses until after Phase 1 fails to reach `local_lb` eliminated hundreds of megabytes of redundant memory writes across tree search. All benchmark sizes verified with identical costs and passing validation.
- The `3a1822d` rows demonstrated that fusing candidate counting and Zobrist hashing into a single memory pass in Phase B of `OptimalPolicy::scan` maintained top performance across all sizes (N=1000 at 5.140s, N=1600 at 22.948s, N=2000 at 46.572s, and N=2200 at 88.148s with pick time 87.2s). All runs passed policy tree validation with identical golden costs.
- The `bb377ce` rows demonstrated that computing the exact number of varying positions ($var\_pos$) in candidate subsets (accounting for constant-grey non-overlapping letters across duplicate-letter words in addition to isograms) and bounding the maximum branching factor by $3^{var\_pos}$ (tightened to $\le 8$ when $overlap\_count=1, var\_pos=2$ and $\le 12$ when $overlap\_count=1, var\_pos=3$) achieved a clean sweep of new all-time records across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 broke the 5-second barrier (**4.937s**, pick 4.7s), N=1600 dropped to **22.251s** (pick 21.8s), N=2000 dropped by 1.24s to **44.774s** (pick 44.3s), and N=2200 dropped by 2.83s to **84.759s** (pick 83.9s). All runs passed policy tree validation with identical golden costs.
- The `0f297b4` rows demonstrated that eliminating intermediate `phase1_guesses` and `phase2_guesses` heap vectors from `SolverScratch` and evaluating active candidates and non-candidate guesses directly in-place during `min_state_val` (Phase 1 scanning `set` directly and Phase 2 scanning bit-unpacked slices directly) eliminated recurring memory allocation and copy churn across DFS branches, achieving a clean sweep of new all-time records across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 down to **4.932s** (pick 4.7s), N=1600 breaking the 22-second barrier (**21.956s**, pick 21.5s), N=2000 down to **44.253s** (pick 43.7s, -0.52s), and N=2200 down to **83.408s** (pick 82.5s, -1.35s). All runs passed policy tree validation with identical golden costs.
- The `7eb5563` rows demonstrated that pre-slicing `g_row`, hoisting the `max_collisions` cutoff calculation outside the inner candidate comparison loop, and using unchecked slice indexing in `quick_bucket_eval` established new all-time records for N=1000 (**4.892s**, breaking below 4.90s for the first time, pick 4.7s) and N=2000 (**44.134s**, pick 43.7s).
- The `d4c8c91` rows demonstrated that fast-pathing in-set candidate guesses achieving the theoretical minimum cost $2n - 1$ (all-singleton partition) directly in `OptimalPolicy::guess_and_cost` for candidate sets $n \le 15$ preserved exact optimal tie-breaking and reduced N=2200 build time to **84.005s** (pick time down to **83.2s**).
- The `e992b06` rows demonstrated that avoiding per-node 243-vector allocations in `build_into` (reusing an array across DFS iterations) and fast-pathing singleton candidates directly without partitioning overhead eliminated >560,000 vector allocations across tree construction, tying the N=1600 record at **21.992s** and delivering **44.243s** at N=2000 while preserving exact policy tree structure.
- The `b338fb4` rows demonstrated that unifying candidate counting across Phase 1 and Phase 2 in `min_state_val` (replacing the `c_len <= 8` linear search over `local_counts` with branchless direct indexing into `counts` and non-empty tracking) eliminated inner loop comparisons and branches across thousands of guess evaluations, setting a new all-time record for N=1000 at **4.856s** (pick 4.7s).
- The `2fa7fd2` rows demonstrated that sorting surviving root guesses in `OptimalPolicy::scan` Phase C by `(exp, orig_idx)` rather than `(lb, exp)` prioritized evaluating well-balanced partitions with lower expected remaining first. This reduced explored states at N=2200 from 24,973 to 23,006 and established a new all-time record of **82.675s** (pick time down to **81.9s**, 1.33s faster than previous best).
- The `f486deb` rows demonstrated that deferring the sorting of `phase2_tuples` in `Solver::min_state_val` until after checking `if local_lb < best_val && self.current_cost_so_far + local_lb < current_global_beta` eliminated unnecessary sorting overhead when Phase 2 is pruned upfront. This established new all-time records for N=1600 (**21.912s**, pick **21.5s**) and N=2000 (**44.085s**, pick **43.7s**), with N=1000 at 4.929s and N=2200 at 83.227s (pick 82.5s). All runs passed policy tree validation with identical golden costs.
- The `4fce4a4` rows demonstrated that dynamically comparing each candidate guess's `orig_idx` against `best_idx` in `OptimalPolicy::scan` tightened the required cutoff to `best_val` (instead of `best_val + 1`) whenever `orig_idx >= best_idx`, since guesses with worse tie-breaker rank cannot beat an existing best solution at the same cost. This pruned unpromising root guesses earlier and achieved a clean sweep of new all-time records across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 at **4.866s** (pick 4.7s), N=1600 dropping to **21.705s** (pick **21.3s**), N=2000 breaking the 44-second barrier down to **43.816s** (pick **43.4s**), and N=2200 dropping to **81.762s** (pick **81.1s**, reducing explored states to 22,592). All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

This achieved a clean sweep of new all-time records across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 down to **4.841s** (pick **4.6s**), N=1600 down to **21.578s** (pick **21.2s**), N=2000 down to **43.498s** (pick **43.1s**), and N=2200 down to **81.259s** (pick **80.6s**, with explored states dropping to **22,550**). All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

Accessing `c_base.add(g)` sequentially ensures 64 consecutive guesses hit the same pre-fetched L1 cache line per candidate. This achieved a monumental sweep of all-time records across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 broke below 4.75s (**4.747s**, pick 4.6s), N=1600 dropped to **21.180s** (pick down to **20.8s**, -0.5s), N=2000 broke the 43s barrier down to **42.698s** (pick down to **42.3s**, -1.0s), and N=2200 shattered the 80s barrier down to **77.516s** (pick down 3.4s to **76.9s**, 3.454s faster overall). All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.
- The `0f4dcc0` rows demonstrated that streaming candidate responses sequentially down `matrix.data_c_g` columns in `compute_max_branching_factor_seq_with_target` (instead of striding across `matrix.data` rows for every guess) eliminated cache line misses across the ~14,000-guess dictionary scan in Pass 2b branching factor evaluations. Because all guesses for a candidate are contiguous in memory, 64 consecutive guess evaluations hit the same pre-fetched L1 cache line per candidate. This delivered the single largest performance leap in recent project history across all benchmark sizes: N=1000 broke below 4 seconds for the first time (**3.857s**, pick **3.7s**, 18.7% faster), N=1600 dropped from 21.180s to **12.300s** (pick down to **12.0s**, **41.9% faster**), N=2000 dropped from 42.698s to **20.696s** (pick down to **20.3s**, **51.5% faster, >2x speedup**), and N=2200 dropped from 77.516s to **30.750s** (pick down to **30.2s**, **60.3% faster, 2.52x speedup!**). All runs passed policy tree validation with invariant golden costs.

- The `caeebcb` rows demonstrated that pooling `counts`, `bucket_hashes`, `p_lbs`, and `child_max_ks` in `SolverScratch` with targeted RAII cleanup (resetting strictly modified indices rather than repeatedly zeroing 4,924 bytes of stack per guess evaluation across 740k+ calls) eliminated over 3.6 GB of stack zeroing overhead. States and guesses evaluated vary between runs of the same commit and overlap between the two commits.
- The `ac0cb8a` rows (branch `fix-capacity-bound`) were run interleaved with `fd4e06f` (main), three runs each, alternating. The new capacity bounds give the same values as the old ones wherever those were valid (`n <= k^2 - k + 2` for one root); the timings agree within noise (medians 3.819s vs 3.824s at N=1000, 36.051s vs 36.076s at N=2340).
- Rows are keyed by `(commit, seed, candidates)`: one commit can have several
  rows, one per size, which is what makes the size-vs-time comparison possible.
